| #240 | **SHOWDOWN RE-SORTS THE ACTION QUEUE ONLY WHEN THE NEXT ACTION IS A MOVE, AND WE RE-SORT ALWAYS — WILL, 2026-08-13.** Found by him reading a diverging card: a turn where all four slots switch, Tyranitar arrives with Sand Stream, and the two engines order the remaining two switches oppositely. His hypothesis was that the sand turns on Excadrill's Sand Rush and makes it suddenly faster, and **the mechanism is exactly right while the direction is inverted — it is OURS that applies it.** `battle.js:2403` guards the re-sort: `if (this.gen >= 8 && (this.queue.peek()?.choice === "move" || this.queue.peek()?.choice === "runDynamax")) { this.updateSpeed(); for (const a of this.queue.list) if (a.pokemon) this.getActionSpeed(a); this.queue.sort(); }`. It runs after EVERY action, but the condition passes only when the NEXT queued action is a move — so while switches are at the head of the queue the speeds are never recomputed and all four keep the values stamped at turn start, before the sand existed. Sand Rush is live (`onModifySpe -> chainModify(2) if sandstorm`) and simply never read. **THE EVIDENCE IS SHOWDOWN'S OWN ORDER, NOT THE DEX**: it puts Blaziken's replacement before Excadrill's, which means Blaziken was the faster body at turn start; we reverse that one pair and nothing happened in between except the weather. Base speeds (Excadrill 88, Blaziken 80) would put Excadrill first either way, so base stats prove nothing here and are not the argument. **THIS IS THE SECOND SIGHTING OF THE SAME RULE, IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION.** #232 closed by moving the Protect `willAct()` scan to the shield's own action *because* the authority re-sorts mid-turn; this row is the other half — the authority does NOT re-sort between switches. So the trigger CONDITION is the fact, and both sites are consequences of it. Fix once, in one place, or the two halves will keep contradicting each other. **AND IT CANNOT BE PATCHED AS "SWITCHES DO NOT SEE THE WEATHER"**: the instant one action in the turn is a move, the re-sort fires and Sand Rush DOES apply to everything still queued, so the same board gives opposite answers depending on whether anybody clicked a move | CLOSED 2026-08-13 — **the trigger landed as the fact, in one place, and the probe was shown RED under BOTH of the wrong engines it replaces.** `_resortTail(from)` in `battleTurn` is the only site that re-derives the remaining queue; its guard is `sdChoiceOf(acts[from].a) === 'move'`, i.e. `peek()?.choice`, and the mega phase's tail sort now goes through it too. `sdChoiceOf` is the mapping from this engine's action kinds to Showdown's `choice` string at module scope, read by the trigger AND by `willAct()` — **so ROADMAP #232's half and this one are now one rule rather than two that happen to agree**; a bare switch maps to `'switch'`, a pivot and `kind:'pass'` map to `'move'` (#232's own finding, reused). **DERIVED ON THE AUTHORITY BEFORE THE ENGINE MOVED**, L50/0EV/31IV/Hardy — Aerodactyl 150, Volcarona 120, Excadrill 108 (216 in sand), Swampert 80, Tyranitar arriving first: four switches end in SUN and the Mold Breaker control is IDENTICAL, while the same board with two of the slots clicking Sunny Day against Rain Dance ends in RAIN and the control reverses it. **The probe carries BOTH rows**, because "never re-sort" passes a switches-only probe and loses #232's mega — break A (always re-sort) makes the switch row read rain, break B (never) makes the move row read sun and ALSO takes down #232's probe and four others. Census 557 -> **558 live / 0 missing**. Measured on a 300-game pair over two releases differing in ONE TOKEN (`ed002956d623` before, `718a7333f27b` after), `--team-store data/team-pool-frozen`, all three planted divergences caught in both: **top-tie-first 98 -> 97 of 260, bottom-tie-first 93 -> 91**, and on the primary arm **exactly one divergence class moved and it is `ordering`** (16 games/15 causes -> 15/14) with every other class unchanged to the instance. `tests/test-engine-diff.js --n 6000` 6000/0, unchanged — a single-hit instrument cannot see a turn order. **TWO ADJACENT DEFECTS WERE FOUND AND DECLARED RATHER THAN FOLDED IN**: `getActionSpeed` re-derives PRIORITY as well as speed (`sim/battle.js:2145` runs ModifyPriority on every re-sort), so this file's claim that only speed is re-derived was wrong and is corrected, while `_pri` stays frozen as a declared defect with no probe; and the `Update` event sorts on the authority's CACHED `pokemon.speed` where `_updateAll` recomputes, which can only reorder berry activations between two bodies whose relative Speed moved with no move queued next |
| #241 | **A GENERIC `-fail` NAMES THE MOVE'S USER AND WE NAME THE TARGET — WILL, 2026-08-13.** Third sighting of one family: he read a card where Meowstic clicks Thunder Wave into an already-poisoned Snorlax, Showdown emits `|-fail|p1a: Meowstic` and we emit `|-fail|p2a: Snorlax`. The two Encore announcements he flagged earlier the same night are the same shape. **THE AUTHORITY'S RULE IS POSITIONAL, NOT PER-MOVE**: every generic failure in `battle-actions.js` is `add("-fail", pokemon)` / `add("-fail", source)` — the mover — and is accompanied by `attrLastMove("[still]")`, which ALSO blanks field 4 of the preceding `|move|` line. The ONLY site naming the target is `pokemon.js:1250`, `add("-fail", this, this.status)`, and it fires solely when the target already carries THE SAME status being applied, always with the status as a third field. Snorlax holds `psn` and Thunder Wave applies `par`, so that branch cannot fire and ours is wrong in the body, in the missing `[still]`, and in the absent third field. **NOT #239, WHICH WAS THE ADJACENT CASE**: that row separated `-immune` from `-fail`; this one is about WHO a `-fail` is attributed to once it is correctly a `-fail`. **SIZED 2026-08-13 AND THE SIZING CORRECTS THE ROW: ATTRIBUTION IS THE SMALL HALF.** Grouped by shape over all 241 distinct causes in `data/game-differential.json` (1,539 games per arm, release `d5ddbedcbc58`), **21 name `-fail`**, in three shapes, and the one this row was opened on is the rarest: (1) **GOOD AS GOLD — 4 causes and the largest single item.** The authority emits `|-immune|<target>|[from] ability: Good as Gold` and we emit a bare `|-fail|<source>`. Derived: `onTryHit` is its ONLY handler, `if (move.category === "Status" && target !== source) { this.add("-immune", target, "[from] ability: Good as Gold"); return null; }` — one carrier in the regulation, **Gholdengo, 3,043 corpus uses**, the second-largest ability in the whole `-fail` group. **THE REFUSAL ITSELF IS CORRECT AND ALREADY IMPLEMENTED** (`medicham2-browser.js` names it at eleven sites and a pinned two-arm measurement confirmed the block); only the LINE is wrong, which is why nothing caught it. (2) **`-immune` vs `-fail` without an ability tag — 3 more causes.** (1) and (2) are the residue of #239, which fixed `setStatus`'s immunity path and did not reach a refusal that happens at `onTryHit` instead. (3) **SHOWDOWN FAILS AND WE ARE SILENT — 8 causes**, the `|-fail|pXa <> |upkeep` shape, where the authority announces a failure we never emit at all. **THE ATTRIBUTION CASE THIS ROW WAS OPENED ON DOES NOT APPEAR IN THE TOP 20** — it is real (read on card 2, verified against `pokemon.js:1250`) and it is rare, so it must not lead the fix. Order: Good as Gold and the `-immune` family first, the silent-failure group second, attribution last. **(1) AND (2) LANDED 2026-08-13; (3) AND THE ATTRIBUTION CASE STAY OPEN.** The sizing was right and the fix was bigger than the row said: a sweep over all **78 legal foe-aimed Status moves x 8 bodies (624 cases)** found **TWELVE action kinds** routing such a move and **FOUR different wrong answers** — `|-fail|` on the MOVER (phaze, reorder, abilitywrite, statrewire), **nothing at all** (typechange, trickitem, abilityswap, boostally, yawn), an **unattributed** `|-immune|` (the pivot switch), and `|-fail|<target>|heal` + `|-fail|<mover>` (healdesc). Two of the twelve were not emission defects at all: **Lock-On and Heal Pulse had NO refusal**, so a Lock-On at a Gholdengo applied the guarantee and a Heal Pulse HEALED it. Fixed as **ONE announcer** — `tryHitRefusal`/`announceTryHitRefusal` — covering only the refusals that resolve at Showdown's onTryHit step, with `announcesWith` read off `onTryHit` through **the same `immuneAttrIn()` reader #239 built for `onSetStatus`** (0 of 7 `statusImmune` rows moved; 1 row in the whole artifact changed; membership printed first — `refusesStatusMoves` has exactly ONE member). Part (2)'s two causes point in OPPOSITE directions: **Mean Look into a Ghost** is refused SILENTLY and fails on the MOVER (`tryTrap` calls `runStatusImmunity("trapped")` with no `message`, sim/pokemon.js:1171), and a **Prankster-boosted status move into a Dark type** is a BARE `|-immune|` on the target (battle-actions.js:573), gated on `!isAlly` as the authority gates it. **THE HEADLINE MOVED BY THREE GAMES AND ONE ARM MOVED THE WRONG WAY, AND THAT IS THE HONEST RESULT**: 1,539-game pinned pool, releases `718a7333f27b` -> `a2857a1bfd70`, top-tie-first 221 -> 218, bottom-tie-first 237 -> 244, `unrelated event mismatch` 33 games/30 causes -> 27/25 with all seven target causes GONE by name, and every other class unmoved except `drag: a different body` 21 -> 25 and `ordering` 42 -> 43 — four of the seven were replaced by a LATER divergence in the same game, three of the four new drag causes naming Gholdengo, which is what removing an early stop looks like. 3,043 corpus uses counts ENTITIES on sheets, not games, and this is a narration fix, so the boards were already agreeing. Census **558 -> 561 live / 0 missing**; three probes shown RED first (`refusesStatusMoves` at 1/12 routes with each wrong route named, `trapsTarget`, `priorityMod` at three arms). **STILL WRONG AND WRITTEN DOWN**: Good as Gold's handler says `target !== source`, so the authority refuses an ALLY-aimed status move into your own Gholdengo (measured on Decorate, Coaching, Skill Swap, Heal Pulse) and every one of those branches is still `_isFoe`-gated here — a REFUSAL defect, not an emission one, deliberately not half-fixed inside an emission pass **(that clause was true when it was written and is now STALE: it became #255 and CLOSED 2026-08-13, where the sweep found NINE wrong branches rather than the four measured here, four of them carrying no refusal at all)**; Role Play and Spite reach `kind:'pass'`, which carries no target to announce against; Heal Pulse's second `|-fail|<mover>` on the ordinary full-HP path; Soundproof/Overcoat announcing bare | (1)+(2) CLOSED — (3) and attribution OPEN, engine DEFECT |
| #242 | **A DURATION-ONLY EFFECT EXPIRES FROM INSIDE THE RESIDUAL WALK AND OUR DERIVED ORDER TABLE CANNOT SEE IT — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"its the same problem of when tailwind officially ends and how that affects leftovers and residual damage"*, said of a card where Showdown runs Leftovers, Leftovers, the Infestation chip and THEN `-sideend` Tailwind, while we announce the Tailwind expiry FIRST. Same family as the Trick Room-end vs sandstorm-chip ordering he called earlier. **CHASING IT FOUND THE HOLE IN THE INSTRUMENT, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE CARD.** `engine/residual_order.js` derives the walk by enumerating effects that own an `onResidual` handler — 42 of them. **Tailwind owns none.** It is in the authority's walk purely because it has a `duration`: `findSideEventHandlers(side, "onSideResidual", "duration")` collects it, and then `battle.js:340` fires the expiry at its SORTED POSITION — `if (eventid === "Residual" && handler.end && handler.state?.duration) { handler.state.duration--; if (!handler.state.duration) { handler.end.call(...); continue; } }`. So every duration-only effect in the format — Tailwind, Trick Room, the screens, Safeguard, Mist, Lucky Chant — expires from inside the residual and is INVISIBLE to the table we derive the order from. The 42-row table is not wrong; it is answering a narrower question than its name suggests, which is the `Dex.forFormat`-is-not-a-legality-filter mistake in a new place. **TWO JOBS, AND THE SECOND IS THE REAL ONE**: place the expiries correctly, and make `residual_order.js` enumerate by `duration` as well as by handler so its row count is the population rather than a subset. **A THIRD DEFECT SITS ON THE SAME LINE**: ours reads `|-sideend|p2: |move: Tailwind` with a BLANK player name where the authority has `p2: B`. **THE POPULATION, DERIVED 2026-08-13 AND FILTERED TO THE REGULATION: 49 effects carry a duration, and 44 of them own NO residual handler of any kind** — so `residual_order.js`'s 42 rows and this set of 44 overlap in FIVE (Encore, Grassy Terrain, Perish Song, Syrup Bomb, Uproar). Every one of the 44 is collected into the walk on the KEY `duration` and expires from inside it. **19 of the 44 announce**, because `removeVolatile` / `removeSideCondition` / `removePseudoWeather` fire `singleEvent("End"/"SideEnd"/"FieldEnd")` and those 19 carry a handler that adds a line: Aurora Veil, Disable, Electric Terrain, Gravity, Light Screen, Lucky Chant, Magic Room, Magnet Rise, Mist, Misty Terrain, Psychic Terrain, Reflect, Safeguard, Tailwind, Taunt, Throat Chop, Trick Room, Wonder Room, Yawn. The other 25 expire silently (Protect, the Guards, the two-turn moves, Helping Hand) and are still ORDER-BEARING, because their `duration--` consumes a position in the same walk. So the fix is 19 lines placed correctly plus 25 silent decrements in the right order, and the instrument must enumerate by `duration` OR handler rather than by handler alone | open — engine DEFECT |
| #243 | **THE LIVE FALLEN COUNT UPDATES AT TURN END, SO A LAST RESPECTS FIRED AFTER AN ALLY DIED THAT TURN IS ONE DEATH LIGHT — WILL, 2026-08-13.** He asked directly: *"does last respects update mid turn if an ally dies first and then last respects hits"*. **In the authority, YES, and the two fallen-count mechanics answer it OPPOSITELY** — which is the part worth holding on to. `Last Respects` evaluates its `basePowerCallback` inside `getDamage` (`battle-actions.js:1375`), i.e. at the moment the move hits, and `side.totalFainted++` happens inside `faintMessages()` (`battle.js:2092`), which `runAction` calls after EVERY action. So an ally that dies earlier in the turn is already counted. `Supreme Overlord` reads the same field ONCE in `onStart` and freezes it in `effectState.fallen`, and never re-reads — so a body dying while Kingambit stands there does nothing for it, and only a Kingambit entering afterwards benefits. **WE MODEL THE SPLIT CORRECTLY AND THE TIMING WRONG**: `att._sf.fainted` is live and `_fallenStuck` is the entry snapshot, exactly right, but `sfA.fainted = fallenCount(...)` runs once per turn in the end-of-turn block, whose own comment says so — *"a mid-turn kill is seen one action late — an approximation, stated"*. Declared rather than hidden, which is the right way to carry a gap, and it is still wrong: Last Respects is **+50 base power per death**, so being one death behind is 100 where the authority says 150, a third of the move off. **INVISIBLE TO EVERY EXISTING TEST**, because the census probe stages the death on a PREVIOUS turn (`"one ally really fainted first 1,101"`) and the whole-game differential pins the dice so the ordering that produces a same-turn kill is rare. The fix is to recompute where the authority does — after each action, not at turn end | CLOSED 2026-08-13 — `docs/ENGINE.md`. `fallenSettle(S)` derives both sides' counters at each of the authority's own moments: at `battleInit` after the roster is stamped, at the top of the action loop beside `flushAfterMoveSpends`/`receiverSweep` and ABOVE the `sideWiped` break, after the last action, and once more in the residual so the Supreme Overlord snapshot includes what the residual killed. One function, four call sites. **Authority measured first** at seed [1,2,3,4]: Houndstone's Last Respects after a Garchomp KOs the Milotic ally earlier in the same turn deals 69 against 36 with the ally alive — x1.917, BP 50 -> 100 — while Iron Head from a Kingambit ALREADY OUT deals 70 against 70, x1.000. This engine read x1.000 on both and now reads x1.980 and x1.000. **The split survived and is asserted**: `ability boostsFromFallen` gained a probe that asserts Supreme Overlord did NOT become live, shown RED under a deliberate break (`_fallenStuck` swapped for `_sf.fainted`) because a probe that asserts a negative passes on the unfixed engine too. Census 561 -> 564 live / 0 missing. Whole-game differential BEFORE (`a2857a1bfd70`) and AFTER (`0c5bb83c5744`), releases verified to differ in exactly one file, same pool, 1219 games, both arms: **220/239 diverged in both, every class row identical to the instance — nothing moved**, which is expected because Mode A's pins rarely produce a same-turn kill followed by a Last Respects, and is not a strength claim. The `Math.min(fainted,5)` at the read site was checked rather than assumed: the authority has NO cap on this move, ours first binds at six fallen allies, a bring-4 game tops out at three, so it is unreachable and is left in place with a comment naming it |

| #251 | **MAG BECOMES A FUTILITY GATE AND STOPS BEING A LEARNED SCORER — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"im thinking of cutting mag back in size to say, no fake out after turn 1, no poison on steel, no status moves into gholdengo, no prio moves when farig is on the board... just very simple rules and not the whole convoluted, protect and let my partner ko the threat."* **THIS FIXES AN ORDERING FAULT AND IS NOT MERELY A SIMPLIFICATION.** A learned MAG prunes what is bad ON AVERAGE and runs BEFORE DODUO, so it deletes precisely the actions that are bad alone and good in combination — Fake Out into a spread, Follow Me covering a setup turn, a weak click that baits a Protect — which is the entire class DODUO exists to find. The safe line is FUTILE (no other action rescues it) versus BAD (conditional, must survive to DODUO). **THE GATE MUST BE DERIVED, NOT WRITTEN, AND ALL FOUR EXAMPLE RULES ARE WRONG AS TYPED**: Fake Out is `activeMoveActions > 1` — first turn OUT, not turn 1 of the game, so a Fake Out user switching in on turn 6 may still use it; Corrosion (Salazzle, Glimmora) poisons Steel; Good as Gold is `breakable` and refuses only when `target !== source`; Armor Tail is one of Farigiraf's THREE abilities and exempts Perish Song, Flower Shield and Rototiller. Category right every time, hand-written form wrong every time — the ban-list-of-four shape, and **a prune list is worse than a ban list because it fails silently**: a deleted option leaves no trace in any output. So the gate asks the ENGINE "does this action produce any effect on this board" rather than holding rules. **OPEN SHEETS DO NOT REMOVE THE NEED**: Fake Out's condition is battle STATE and appears on no sheet, and the sheet stops being true — six legal moves change an ability mid-battle plus 11 Mold Breaker carriers and a Mummy, and Knock Off has 4,078 corpus uses. It reads the LIVE BOARD, which on turn one is the same object and by turn four is not. **BUILDABLE NOW — the only stage of the pipeline with no fitted parameters, so the quarantine does not block it.** Needs a counter for what it pruned and a check that a pruned action really was a no-op: an over-pruning gate deletes a good play and nothing catches it, because both sides of every comparison share the blind spot | open — search |
| #252 | **THE FUTILITY GATE CARRIES A DECLARED PREDICTION, AND WILL BROKE THE SAFETY ARGUMENT THE SAME NIGHT — 2026-08-13.** *"of course there is forbidden tech of clicking a prio move when farig is on the board because youre making a prediction of it switching out."* The rule in #251 was stated as "no action rescues it", and that quantified over MY PARTNER'S actions only. Quantified over the OPPONENT'S as well, most of the gate's membership stops being unconditional: **a move follows the SLOT, so switching the slot rescues it.** Priority into an Armor Tail Farigiraf is dead only while that Farigiraf is still there when the move resolves; the same holds for Toxic into a Steel and any status into Gholdengo. **TWO HALVES, ONE ASSUMPTION-FREE**: the UNCONDITIONAL half fails on the USER's own state (Fake Out out of position, a Choice lock, no PP) and asserts nothing; the OCCUPANT-DEPENDENT half asserts *"they will not switch"*. Three of the four original examples are in the second half. The gate may still cut them — it is usually right and the expected cost is small in a format where the read is rare — but it is a PREDICTION and must be recorded as one. **DEFERRED BY WILL'S DECISION, NOT MISSED**: *"id rather not engage with those type of scenarios at the moment but make a note of it for things to possibly adjust much much later into the project."* When taken up, the home is not the gate: an action that is dead under one opponent action and live under another is a COLUMN of the equilibrium matrix, not a row to delete before coordination | deferred by decision — search |
| #253 | **THE WORD "ENDGAME" IS CLAIMED BY TWO MODELS IN OUR OWN LEDGER, AND THAT IS WHY THE ARCHITECTURE WAS HARD TO HOLD — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"wait didnt we have dusknoir as the endgame guy? its hard to keep track."* He is right and the confusion is ours, not his memory. `docs/MODELS.md:1451` scopes **DUSK** as the endgame TABLEBASE — exact, precomputed offline, shipped as a lookup because it cannot be solved under a turn timer. SLOWKING's **Job:** line in the same file reads *"the endgame — tell you the equilibrium-best move (and win %) on a live position"*, which is not distinguishable in wording. **THE SPLIT, NOW WRITTEN DOWN**: DUSK is small endgames, EXACT, offline then looked up; SLOWKING is any position, approximate (an equilibrium over ESTIMATED values), live per turn; HYPNO is about the OPPONENT rather than the position and is a deliberate deviation FROM SLOWKING. **Only DUSK can say "guaranteed"** — which means Will's own description of the late-game stage (*"which ones lead to a guaranteed win... hand these scenarios to miltank"*) is DUSK's job and was filed under HYPNO. Fixed in `docs/MODELS.md` and `docs/ALAKAZAM-v2-spec.md`. **AND THE MATRIX IS KEPT EVEN IF GREEDY WINS**: *"id still want slowking matrix even if greedy wins right? because then if we play opponents regularly we can adapt and become unexploitable."* Keeping it is right and is already ADR-003's position — VGC-Bench's policy beats a Worlds competitor and is ~100% exploitable, so winning on average and being safe are different axes. **ONE CORRECTION TO THE REASONING**: adapting to an opponent makes you MORE exploitable, not less. Nash never adapts and cannot be beaten in the long run; deviating to punish a habit opens a hole in you. What repeated play actually buys is the other direction — a FIXED policy degrades because they learn YOU, and greedy is a fixed mapping. The matrix is not the adaptation mechanism; it is the floor you measure your deviations against, and HYPNO is the deviation | open — direction |
| #254 | **EVERY HAZARD LANDS ON THE SIDE THAT LAID IT IN THE OFFLINE BOARD, AND THE LIVE PATH IS RIGHT — SO THE FIT AND THE GAME DISAGREE. FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE CHASING #249; SPLIT OUT AND VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY.** `engine/board.js` `noteMove` ends with `if (move.sideCondition) board.startSide(side, move.sideCondition, ...)` — `side` is the MOVER's, unconditionally. **DERIVED FROM THE FORMAT, NOT RECALLED: of the 11 legal side-condition moves in this regulation, SEVEN are `target: allySide` and FOUR are `target: foeSide`** — Stealth Rock, Spikes, Sticky Web, Toxic Spikes. The seven (Reflect, Light Screen, Aurora Veil, Safeguard, Tailwind, Quick Guard, Wide Guard) are correct BY ACCIDENT, because the mover's side is the right answer for them. The four hazards are recorded on the layer's own side, which is the side they can never be on. **THIS IS NOT #249 AND MUST NOT BE CLOSED WITH IT.** That row is the SEED carrying no hazards into a playout — a missing feature, and a rollout that ignores hazards is merely blind. This is a feature that is PRESENT AND INVERTED, and it sits in the board the FIT reads: `engine/board.js` is the offline board, the live path reads Showdown's `|-sidestart|` and gets it right. **That is FITTING ENVIRONMENT AND PLAYING ENVIRONMENT MUST MATCH, broken in a new place** — the same shape as MAG fitted with the sheet visible and played without it, and MACHAMP trained under broken mega. A model fitted on a board where Stealth Rock hurts its own team learned something about a game nobody plays. **SIZE, AND IT IS SMALL AND THAT IS NOT THE ARGUMENT**: 259 corpus uses across the four (Stealth Rock 168, Sticky Web 42, Toxic Spikes 34, Spikes 15) against 16,074 for Tailwind alone. A wrong value is worse than a missing one at equal volume, because nothing downstream can tell it is wrong. **THE FIX IS DERIVED, NOT A LIST OF FOUR**: read `move.target` and place the condition on the far side when it is `foeSide`. A hand-written hazard list here would be the ban-list-of-four shape a third time, and a hazard added next regulation would silently land on the wrong side again. **THREE SITES, NOT ONE, AND THEY ARE CURRENTLY WRONG TOGETHER — WHICH IS THE ONLY REASON NOTHING HAS BROKEN.** Read 2026-08-13 before anyone touches it: `engine/board.js:2675` WRITES with `board.startSide(side, ...)`; `:3311` READS with `board.hasSide(side, m.sideCondition)` to set `deadSide` ("this move cannot work, it is already up"); `:3343` READS the same way for `alreadyUp`, which gates `setupTurns`. All three take the mover's side, so today they are SELF-CONSISTENT and the error cancels — a hazard is written to the wrong side and then looked for on that same wrong side. **FIXING THE WRITE ALONE PRODUCES A WORSE BUG THAN THE ONE IT FIXES**: the hazard would land on the foe's side while both readers keep looking at the mover's, so `deadSide` would never fire for a hazard and the model would re-lay Stealth Rock every turn believing it was not up, crediting itself `setupTurns` each time. **SO IT IS ONE HELPER, NOT THREE EDITS** — a single `sideFor(board, side, move)` that answers "whose side does this condition live on" from `move.target`, called by the write and by both reads. Whose side a condition lands on is a FACT, and three call sites must not each decide it independently; that is the rule this repo has broken most expensively. `const foeSide = side === 'p1' ? 'p2' : 'p1'` is already an idiom at five other places in the same file, so the helper has a shape to match. **A PROBE MUST COVER BOTH DIRECTIONS**: a hazard laid by p1 is readable by p1 as "up on the far side" AND must still set `deadSide` when p1 tries to lay it again. A probe that only checks placement passes on an engine that placed it right and can no longer see it. **CLOSED 2026-08-13 BY MEASURE, AND THE ROW UNDERSTATED THE BLAST RADIUS BY SIX SITES.** `sideFor(side, move)` lives in `engine/board.js` beside the class, is exported, and is called by NINE sites: the write at `noteMove`, the two reads (`deadSide`, `alreadyUp`), and **six verbatim copies of the same mover's-side derivation found by ENGINE's sweep — `engine/fit_policy.js:793` (THE FIT ITSELF), `joint_rows.js`, `branch_recall.js`, `corpus_shift.js`, `feature_coverage.js`, `redirect_audit.js`.** Fixing only the three in `board.js` would have left the fit reading a board where a hazard hurts the team that laid it, which is the whole defect wearing a different file name. **ONE CORRECTION TO THIS ROW, MEASURED RATHER THAN REPEATED: "crediting itself `setupTurns` each time" IS WRONG.** None of the four hazards carries a `condition.duration` (Reflect 5, Tailwind 4, the hazards none), so `dur()` returns 0 and `setupTurns` is 0 for them under every variant. The observable movement is `deadSide` alone; the `alreadyUp` site is routed through the helper for the one-fact-one-function rule and binds today only on the seven `allySide` moves, where `sideFor` is the identity. **THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE FIX IS THE GUARD'S.** `tests/test-feature-semantics.js` was STRUCTURALLY BLIND to this: run under both boards with the old fixture, **0 of 76 hashed columns moved**, because `engine/feature_fixture.js` contained **zero hazard clicks** and pre-set only `reflect` and `tailwind` — both `allySide`, exactly the half the fix leaves alone. A new board, `hazards-already-up`, puts `stealthrock`/`spikes` up on p2 and `stickyweb`/`toxicspikes` up on p1 so the flip is exercised in BOTH directions, and `deadSide` now moves `b984c210828d -> d1be4f95d589` with nothing else moving. **RED PROOFS, all three by compiling a pre-#254 `board.js` in memory and injecting it — the tree was never reverted:** `tests/test-hazard-side.js` 5 passed / 3 failed at HEAD and 11/0 after; the semantics guard's new clause names all six wrong candidates; and reverting ONE of the six sweep sites fails the ninth-site clause by file name. `tests/test-mechanics.js` **565 live / 0 missing**, unmoved, as expected — the whole-game differential exercises `medicham2`, not the feature board. **AND THE REFIT VERDICT IS NO, MEASURED: THE FIT IS BIT-IDENTICAL AND THIS WAS ONLY EVER A LIVE-PLAY DEFECT.** The whole fit corpus was replayed through `FP.decisionsFor` under both boards — **9,226 games, 237,052 decisions, 1,731,851 candidate vectors, 0 errored, identical decision key set — and 0 decisions, 0 games and 0 feature vectors move.** The reason is not thin exposure alone (24 hazard clicks: SR 19, Toxic Spikes 2, Sticky Web 2, Spikes 1; 258 decisions carry a hazard candidate) but an ISOMORPHISM, constructed rather than argued: `noteMove` wrote to the mover's side and both readers read the mover's side, so the old board is the new one with the two sides RELABELLED, and every offline-derived read agrees. **The mismatch is against a state that did NOT come from `noteMove` — which is the LIVE path.** `engine/magnemite.js:647` takes the side straight off `|-sidestart|` and calls `noteMove(..., worked=false)`, so on the pre-#254 board a hazard laid by p1 read **`deadSide` p1=0, p2=1** — the LAYER re-lays Stealth Rock forever believing it is not up, and the VICTIM refuses to lay its own believing it is. Fixed: p1=1, p2=0. Same probe, offline arm: identical under both boards for all 11 moves. **So MAG was fitted where `deadSide` was effectively right and PLAYED where it was backwards** — fitting-and-playing-environment-must-match broken in a new place, and closed in the one direction that costs no refit | **CLOSED 2026-08-13** |
| #255 | **GOOD AS GOLD REFUSES ALLY-AIMED STATUS MOVES IN THE AUTHORITY AND OURS ONLY REFUSES FOES — 2026-08-13, LEFT BY THE #241 PASS RATHER THAN HALF-FIXED.** The handler's condition is `move.category === "Status" && target !== source`, so it refuses anything that is not the holder ITSELF — including a partner's Decorate, Coaching, Skill Swap or Heal Pulse, measured on the authority. Every branch here is `_isFoe`-gated, so a Gholdengo accepts support from its own side that Showdown refuses. **THIS IS A REFUSAL DEFECT, NOT AN EMISSION ONE**, which is exactly why it was correct to leave it: #241 parts (1) and (2) were a NARRATION pass, and folding a behaviour change into them would have made a three-game differential move unattributable. **AND IT IS A REAL SET, NOT A CURIOSITY** — Gholdengo is the single carrier and ally-aimed support into your own Gholdengo is ordinary doubles play. Fix it with its own before/after. **CLOSED 2026-08-13, AND THE SWEEP DOUBLED THE SCOPE THE ROW STATED.** Derived first: one carrier in the regulation (Gholdengo, filtered walk) and `refusesStatusMoves` has EXACTLY ONE member over the whole tag corpus (`abilities:goodasgold`, 3,043 uses), printed before anything was wired. The authority was measured one battle per route — a supporter aiming at its own partner — and **26 routes all print `|-immune|p1b: Gholdengo|[from] ability: Good as Gold`**, including the untargetable ones (Life Dew, Howl, Heal Bell, Perish Song, Teeter Dance, Corrosive Gas); **NOT refused in the same sweep: Tailwind, Light Screen, Safeguard, Trick Room, Misty Terrain, Haze**, because a side and a field are not a body. Fixed by making the one announcer SIDE-BLIND (`tryHitRefusal` already carried `t!==m`, which IS `target !== source`) and calling it PER BODY in the spread branches, so Howl still lifts the user and Perish Song still marks the other three; `pranksterBlocked` keeps its own foe gate inside that function because the authority gates it there, so this widens one ability and nothing else. New counter `MEDSEEN.tryHitRefusedAlly`. **THE PROBE WAS WRONG BEFORE THE ENGINE WAS, TWICE**: it read the `|move|` line as an effect (it names the target in field 4), and — the one that mattered — its exemption arm was a self-aimed Nasty Plot, which routes to `kind:'setup'`, **a branch that never asks about a refusal**, so deleting the `t===m` exemption outright left the probe GREEN. Every self route now goes through a branch this change edited. Red proof in both directions: exemption deleted -> `564/1` naming all four self routes; `_isFoe` restored on `boostally` alone -> `564/1` naming `decorate`, with the pre-existing foe-side Decorate probe still LIVE. Census **564 -> 565 live / 0 missing**. Whole-game differential, 1,220-game pinned pair, both arms, releases `0c5bb83c5744` -> `9b25e830df49` verified to differ in ONE file: top **254 -> 253**, bottom **294 -> 293**, `unrelated event mismatch` **30/26 -> 29/25** with every other class identical to the instance, and ONE cause gone by name — `|-immune|p2a|[from]goodasgold <> |-singleturn|p2a|helpinghand`, a real game where the authority refused a Helping Hand at a Gholdengo and we applied the mark. No new causes; normalised lines compared rose 1,507,925 -> 1,509,557, which is what removing an early stop looks like. **ONE GAME PER ARM IS THE INSTRUMENT'S REACH, NOT THE FIX'S SIZE** — the swarm aims 828 of ~46,700 clicks at an ally across 1,220 games and only the fraction landing on the one carrier can exercise this. `test-engine-diff.js --n 6000`: 6000 agreed, 0 disagreed, both corners | **CLOSED 2026-08-13** |

| #259 | **ONE FROZEN BODY THAWS WITH AN ANNOUNCEMENT AND THE OTHER THAWS SILENTLY, ON THE SAME TURN — WILL, 2026-08-13, READING CARD 2.** *"for some reason we announce toxapex thaw but not incin thaw."* Both p1 bodies are frozen on turn 1; on turn 2 the authority emits `|-curestatus|p1a: Toxapex|frz|[msg]` AND `|-curestatus|p1b: Incineroar|frz|[msg]`. Ours emits the first and not the second, then plays Incineroar's Parting Shot normally — **so the thaw HAPPENS and only the line is missing.** **THE CODE READ DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT AND THAT IS WHY THIS IS A ROW RATHER THAN A FIX.** One branch handles both bodies — `if(m.status==='frz'){m.frzTurns=(m.frzTurns||0)+1; if(m.frzTurns>=3||rng()<0.25){m.status=''; if(TR)TR.cure(m,'frz',...);} else {...}}` — both enter with `frzTurns` 0, both draw the same pinned roll, both should reach `TR.cure`. Nothing in the branch distinguishes p1a from p1b. **THE SHAPE IS ONE THIS PROJECT HAS HISTORY WITH**: Toxapex is slot **a** and Incineroar is slot **b**, and *"mega evolution fired in self-play only from the LEFT slot"* is the same presentation. | #260 | **"EVERY SECONDARY FIRES" SELF-CANCELS FOR ANY EFFECT WITH AN ESCAPE ROLL, SO THE BOTTOM ARM GUARANTEES FREEZE AND GUARANTEES THAW — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"bro guaranteed freeze in the lower bound is so broken"*, then, one message later, *"but i guess we get guaranteed thaw?"* — both right, and together they name a property of the instrument nobody had stated. `makeArm` pins `random(den)` to **0** in the bottom arm, and `chance = (num, den) => random(den) < num`, so **every** `randomChance` returns true. The freeze secondary (`randomChance(1,10)`) fires AND the thaw check inside `frz.onBeforeMove` (`randomChance(1,4)`) also fires, so a body is frozen and cures on its very next move **without ever losing a turn**. The bottom arm therefore never exercises frozen-turn behaviour at all, while appearing to guarantee it. **THE EFFECT IS NOT UNIFORM AND THAT IS THE POINT**: an effect with ONE roll compounds (paralysis is applied AND fully paralyses every turn; confusion is applied AND self-hits), an effect with an ESCAPE roll cancels (freeze). *"The worst corner"* is therefore the wrong mental model for this arm — it is *"every branch taken"*, which is the worst case only when there is one branch. **NOT A DEFECT IN THE PIN**, which is doing exactly what it says; it is a COVERAGE claim that needs narrowing, and the arm's own description (*"every secondary fires"*) is what misleads. **AND A LATENT COLLISION FOUND WHILE CHECKING IT**: `random(m)` returns the damage index when `m === DAMAGE_ROLL_SIDES` (16), so any `randomChance(x, 16)` would be read as a damage roll rather than a chance. Derived: **no legal move in this regulation has a 1-in-16 secondary**, so it is latent today and would surface silently the day one is added | open — measure |
| #261 | **THE GAME LETS A FROZEN BODY THAW ITSELF BY ATTACKING AND THIS ENGINE DOES NOT, BECAUSE `flags.defrost` HAS NO REPRESENTATION ANYWHERE HERE — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"we need to test that the moves that auto thaw you out (either by the user or getting hit by it) actually thaw."* He named both routes and **the format has both while this engine has one.** Derived, filtered to the regulation: **`flags.defrost` — the USER thaws when it USES the move — has five members: Burn Up, Flare Blitz, Matcha Gotcha, Scald, Scorching Sands.** `thawsTarget` — the TARGET thaws on being HIT — has **three**: Matcha Gotcha, Scald, Scorching Sands. They are different rules over overlapping sets. **`grep -c defrost` RETURNS 0 IN BOTH `engine/medicham2-browser.js` AND `data/tags.json`.** The authority's `frz.onBeforeMove` opens `if (move.flags["defrost"] && !(move.id === "burnup" && !pokemon.hasType("Fire"))) return;` — the frozen body simply moves. Ours has no such clause: the thaw branch reads only `m.frzTurns>=3||rng()<0.25`, so a frozen Incineroar clicking Flare Blitz waits on the 25% roll instead of thawing and attacking. **AND THE TAG WE DO HAVE IS A COLLAPSED PAIR, WHICH IS TASK #1'S SHAPE A SECOND TIME**: `data/tags.json` tags **all five** as `thawsTarget`, so the label is wrong about two of its own members. Burn Up and Flare Blitz do NOT thaw a target — they defrost their user — and that is **masked today because both are Fire and `frz.onDamagingHit` cures on ANY Fire hit** (`move.type === "Fire" && move.category !== "Status" && move.id !== "polarflare"`), so the right thing happens for the wrong reason and no test can tell. Only the three non-Fire members need a `thawsTarget` tag at all; the Fire ones are covered by type. **THE TITLE OF THIS ROW WAS ORIGINALLY A CLAIM ABOUT POKEMON RATHER THAN ABOUT US, AND WILL READ IT THAT WAY** (2026-08-13: *"no frozen bodies can thaw itself if they use certain moves"* — correcting what he took to be a claim that the mechanic does not exist). It does exist, in five moves; what is missing is OUR half. A row heading that cannot be told apart from a statement about the game is the same hazard CLAUDE.md names for illegal entities, one level up, and it cost a round trip here. **THE FIX IS A SPLIT, NOT AN ADDITION**: a `thawsUser` tag parsed off `flags.defrost` with the Burn-Up-needs-Fire-type exception carried as a param, and `thawsTarget` narrowed to the moves that actually do it. **PROBE BOTH ROUTES AND THE FIRE CONFOUND**: a frozen user clicking Flare Blitz (thaws, no tag needed for the target), a frozen target hit by Scald (thaws, non-Fire, needs the tag), and a frozen target hit by a Fire move with neither flag (thaws by type alone) — without the third arm a probe cannot tell the type route from the tag route. **DONE, 2026-08-13.** Census **567 -> 570 live / 0 missing**. `thawsUser` parsed off `flags.defrost` (5 members) carrying `requiresUserType` read from Burn Up's own `onTryMove` through the SAME reader `spendsOwnType.requires` uses — one implementation, two callers — and `thawsTarget` narrowed to `m.thawsTarget` (3 members). The engine clause sits ABOVE the counter tick because the authority's `return` spends no turn and draws no die. Three probes in `tests/test-mechanics.js`, two of them shown MISSING against the shipped engine first; four green-then-red demonstrations in `tests/probe_red_demo.js`, including the target route's TYPE clause, which nothing had ever shown red on its own. Whole-game differential `b4746a1e44a7 -> 652b971cb70d`, 978 games: no count moved in any arm, and the class `-curestatus field 4` DISAPPEARS from the bottom corner — the attribution now reads `[from] move: flareblitz` where it wrote `[msg]`. `test-engine-diff.js --n 6000`: 0/6000 both corners | done — `docs/ENGINE.md`, `docs/MEDICHAM-SPRINT-NOTES.md` |
| #262 | **A MIDDLE ARM WITH EVENT-ADDRESSED DICE, BECAUSE BOTH PINNED CORNERS PRODUCE GAMES NOBODY PLAYS — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"is it possible at all for us to run a middle bound? where things can miss, and secondary chances have a chance to proc? to get some more realistic scenarios to test... otherwise paralysis ends the mons usefulness... thats why games take years."* **He is right about the mechanism and it is measurable**: the bottom arm pins every `randomChance` true (see #260), so the full-paralysis roll fires EVERY turn and a paralysed body never moves again — which is a large part of why the median game hits the 12-turn cap when a real one ends at **7**. **WHY RANDOM DICE ARE NOT THE ANSWER, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE DESIGN PROBLEM**: the arms are pinned for SYNCHRONISATION, not caution. Both engines must give the same answer to the same question or a divergence is a dice disagreement rather than a rule disagreement, and they make different numbers of draws in different orders, so a shared seeded stream desynchronises on turn one. A constant is the only mapping that survives that. **THE WAY OUT IS TO ADDRESS THE DIE BY THE EVENT RATHER THAN BY POSITION IN A SEQUENCE**: `hash(turn, attacker slot, move id, effect name) % 100 < chance`. Order-independent, so the engines agree without being in lockstep; some Rock Slides miss and some flinch; paralysis fires near 1-in-4 instead of every turn; games END. Across thousands of events the observed rates approach the printed ones, which also makes it the natural home for the rate work in #196/#28. **THE COST, STATED**: the two engines must NAME the event identically — same move id, same turn, same target — or the hashes differ and every roll disagrees. The normaliser already maps ids, so it is checkable, and it must be ASSERTED rather than assumed: a probe that plants a known event on both sides and compares the drawn value. **AND IT INHERITS #260**: an effect with an escape roll must not get "true" for both the apply and the escape, which a hash gives for free and a constant never can | **ENGINE HALF LANDED 2026-08-13 — the engine names the event; the AUTHORITY half is still unwired and measures 0.0%.** medicham2 now carries a module-level event address written at four sites (top of the turn, top of the action, the announcement, the step driver) and `midEventDice`/`midEventLog` export it; `tests/test-middle-identity.js` DIFFS the two engines' address logs over 900 real games. Measured, medicham2-matched: **acc 99.7%, dmg 98.8%, crit 98.5%, sec 91.0% — 98.3% pooled over 1,058 events**, floored per category (pooling hid a break: deleting the per-target write left the pool at 91.2% over a floor of 90). Four deliberate breaks, three RED, and the fourth GREEN is itself the finding — the announcement-site write is redundant over 900 games and is now COUNTED rather than trusted. **The row cannot close on ENGINE's side alone.** `game_differential.js` builds its address inside the function it installs as `battle.prng.random`, and `Battle#random` is `this.prng.random(m,n)` — so `this` there is the PRNG and every authority address is `<seed>|undefined|<cat>|-|-|<nth>`. The test computes BOTH streams on every draw and asserts the degenerate one stays under 5%, so the arm cannot be switched on by mistake. Owed in that file: capture `this.battle` in `midWrapShowdown`'s `around()`, and clear `MID_NTH` per game beside the existing `midReset()`. **Three category mislabels fell out of the diff by counting, one of them a standing ROADMAP #222 gap**: the move's own secondaries loop was on the generic stream (28 authority `sec` draws against ZERO from us), and King's Rock and Dire Claw likewise. **What is NOT solved and is not claimed**: `nth` is a repeat counter, so a differing draw count at one address still parts after entry 0 — 457 of 2,029 events are `nth > 0` and that is printed every run | open — measure (authority half) |
| #263 | **THE STORE HAS NO SPREADS AT ALL, SO A REPLAY DIFFERENTIAL CANNOT ADJUDICATE DAMAGE OR SPEED — WILL, 2026-08-13, AND HE TALKED ME OUT OF LEADING WITH IT.** I proposed running `engine/replay_differential.js` against real games as the realistic instrument. *"we dont know the sps on games that actually happened tho"*, then *"so the damage and speed would be off."* **Measured on `data/games.bo3.jsonl`: 4,001 games, 47,856 sheet bodies, EVERY ONE `"evs": null`, ZERO with a real spread.** An open team sheet shows species, ability, item, moves and nature — never the stat points. **SPEED IS THE WORSE HALF AND THAT IS THE PART I MISSED**: damage being off produces a wrong number, but speed being off produces a wrong TURN ORDER, which is categorical — every line after it diverges for a reason that is not a rule. `replay_differential.js`'s own header already makes this argument about reconstructing CHOICES (*"a reconstruction that guesses is worse than no test, because a guess that happens to be wrong looks exactly like an engine defect"*) and does not apply it to the spreads. **THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS IS CONTROL, NOT REALISM**: the synthetic differential's spreads are unknown AND IDENTICAL ON BOTH SIDES, so a divergence can only be a rule; a replay's spreads are unknown AND DIFFERENT from whatever the player actually brought, so a divergence may be either. **Unknown-but-identical is fine; unknown-and-wrong is not.** So the replay differential keeps a NARROWER job — sequences and categorical facts independent of exact numbers (did that ability fire, did that switch happen, was that move legal) — and cannot arbitrate a KO or a speed tie, which is most of what a game turns on. This is ROADMAP #68 restated with its consequence: it is NARROWED by the nature and can never be closed by it. **Do not lead with replay; build #262 instead**, which is synthetic and therefore controls its own spreads | open — measure |
| #264 | **A 100-ACCURACY MOVE CAN MISS, AND THE DIFFERENTIAL ASSERTS THAT IT CANNOT — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"i mean even if a move is 100 accuracy it could still miss due to evasion, bright powder, sand veil, etc."* **He is right and the population is larger than the three he named.** Derived and filtered to the regulation: **3 items** (Bright Powder, Wide Lens, Zoom Lens), **6 abilities** (Sand Veil, Snow Cloak, Compound Eyes, Hustle, Tangled Feet, Wonder Skin) and **3 moves** (Double Team, Minimize, Sweet Scent) touch accuracy or evasion. The authority keeps `accuracy === true` (cannot miss) strictly apart from `accuracy === 100` (rolls, after boosts and modifiers): `hitStepAccuracy` runs the `Accuracy` event and the evasion stages ONLY when it is not `true`, so a printed-100 move into a +2 evasion body is a **60% move**. **WHAT IS CONFIRMED HERE**: `hitChance` does the stage arithmetic, WIRE 129 fixed the bug where `accuracy` and `evasion` mapped to NULL so every Double Team silently vanished, `evasionIgnored` is counted, and the one place a 100 becomes never-miss is documented and CORRECT — rain Thunder, where Showdown itself writes `move.accuracy = true` and the artifact lossily records 100. **WHAT IS NOT CONFIRMED, AND THAT IS THE ROW**: that a printed-100 move into a boosted-evasion body actually misses HERE, at the right rate. The mechanism being present is not evidence it fires — this project's founding lesson — and no probe covers it. **AND THE CLAIM IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN COSMETIC**: `game_differential.js` asserts *"a 100-accuracy move HITS"* as a PIN CLAIM on both corners. If evasion is live, that claim is false whenever a Bright Powder or a Sand Veil is on the field, and the arm would be asserting something the game does not do. **PROBE SHAPE**: a printed-100 move into a body at +0 evasion (never misses), the same into +2 (misses ~40% of the time), and the same into a Bright Powder holder — three arms, because two cannot separate the stage arithmetic from the item modifier **CLOSED 2026-08-13 — AND HALF THE ROW WAS ALREADY TRUE, WHICH IS WHY MEASURING CAME FIRST.** The OUTCOME was never wrong: `hitChance` has always returned the FINAL accuracy and the roll sites have always gated on that, so a printed-100 Ice Beam into a +2 body missed 38.3% here against the authority's 38.8%, and into a Bright Powder holder 9.7% against 10.3%. What was missing was the PROBE, and there are now two — a three-armed RATE probe (a pinned roll cannot tell 60 from 99, and the stage route and the item route are different code) plus an Aerial Ace control that must also be shown LANDING. **THE REAL DEFECT WAS THE DRAW**, wrong in both directions: the attack and stat-change sites gated on `acc < 100`, so a Coil or a Wide Lens (final 133 / 110) stopped drawing where the authority draws, and the two STATUS sites drew unconditionally including on the Infinity a Poison-type Toxic produces, where the authority draws nothing. `accMustRoll(acc)` is now one answer for all four sites and it is `isFinite(acc)`. **THE AMENDMENT'S CHEAP PREDICATE WAS BUILT AND IS A MEASURED REGRESSION** — middle-arm VOID 131/171 before, **133** with the predicate (worse than doing nothing, because it took draws away from the status sites), **100** with the authority's rule; usable games 40 -> 71. And the speed premise does not survive measurement: the skip never avoided the pipeline (`hitChance` runs unconditionally and always did — the guard sat underneath it), and three interleaved rounds of 6,000 turns put the three variants inside each other's round-to-round spread. Census **565 -> 567 live / 0 missing**; four deliberate breaks shown RED. The corners cannot see this and that is now measured rather than suspected: two frozen releases differing in exactly one file, 200 games, top 53/171 and bottom 59/171 **before and after**. `game_differential.js` PIN_CLAIMS need a re-wording (they say "a 100-accuracy move HITS" and assert a FINAL accuracy of 100) — reported, not edited, since MEASURE owns that file. | closed 2026-08-13 — `docs/ENGINE.md` |
| #265 | **FIXTURES WERE DECLARING SETS THE GAME WOULD REFUSE, AND NOTHING IN THE REPOSITORY ASKED — CLOSED 2026-08-13 BY MEASURE.** CLAUDE.md's cardinal rule is that no Pokémon, item, ability or move outside the regulation may be NAMED, in *"every example, every illustration and every derived result"* — and it was being broken inside the tests, where it is worse than in prose: a fixture built on a set that cannot exist measures a game we do not play, and every number it produces inherits that. **BOTH KNOWN INSTANCES WERE FOUND IN PASSING RATHER THAN BY A CHECK**, which is the argument: `engine/feature_fixture.js` gave Venusaur a ROCKY HELMET (banned here since 2026-08-04) and it was spotted by an agent doing #254; seven illegal learnset pairs reached `data/fixture-learnset-baseline.json` because an agent sent to repair one faint happened to run the validator. **THE VERDICT IS `TeamValidator#validateTeam`'s AND NOTHING RE-IMPLEMENTS IT.** Will: *"i thought we were using showdowns team validator as the ultimate legality test."* The first plan was species `isNonstandard`, then the ability against the species list, then the item, then `checkCanLearn` per move — four hand-written rules standing in for a table Showdown maintains, which is FACTS ARE GLOBAL broken inside the file written to enforce a rule. A piecemeal check only finds the rules somebody thought to write; the validator carries the 66-point budget, the 32-per-stat cap, the item clause and every ban the mod adds next. `engine/fixture_legality.js` therefore calls `champions_sim.checkLegal` — one shared validator instance, five proved-clean padding slots — and prints the authority's own sentences. **THE POPULATION, DERIVED RATHER THAN LISTED: 334 .js files, 627 set declarations in 26 files, 227 distinct sets, 55 rejected, 41 distinct verdicts.** Four scanner rules each went in after it was caught being wrong on a real file: a literal counts as an entity only if it normalises to that entity's own id (`dex.species.get('p2')` answers Porygon2 and `p2` here is a SIDE); moves come only from ARRAY literals (`hit('vaporeon','icebeam')` clicks the move at that body, not from it — three false accusations); helpers are taken at TOP LEVEL only (`test-mechanics.js` redeclares `run`/`hit`/`at` in dozens of probe callbacks and a file-global name set paired an ability stamped on the ATTACKER with the DEFENDER); and a set is declared either as an object literal or by MUTATION, which is 64 real sets in `test-protocol-trace.js`. **507 construction sites carry no literal and are outside the population, correctly** — a fixture that derives its bodies from the format cannot type a name that does not exist. **THE GATE IS `tests/test-fixture-legality.js`, ratcheted on `data/fixture-legality-baseline.json`, keyed on the VALIDATOR'S OWN SENTENCE** so the same illegal declaration moved to a new scenario is the same defect. Six clauses: a population floor (a scanner that finds nothing passes everything below it), no new verdict, the baseline may only SHRINK, every entry states a kind and a written reason, a stray literal that names nothing in the format (`'dampro'` for Damp Rock at `test-protocol-trace.js:131` — the item resolves to nothing and the fixture silently runs without it), and a self-check that the ratchet still discriminates. **SHOWN RED THREE WAYS**: re-planting the real Rocky Helmet fails naming `engine/feature_fixture.js:87`; two invented baseline entries fire the stale-allowance clause; one with a bad kind fires the reason clause. **SIX REPAIRS, ALL IN MEASURE'S OWN FIXTURE, COVERAGE MEASURED ACROSS EVERY ONE**: Rocky Helmet → Miracle Seed (4 sites), Electric Seed → Mental Herb, Throat Spray → White Herb, Grimmsnarl's Thunder Wave → Taunt, and Hippowdon's WEATHER BALL, which no legal sand or Rock body in this regulation learns — the flip moved to a new board `sand-weather-ball` rather than displacing anything, after an in-place Heliolisk swap was tried and MEASURED to thin `allyHit` 4→3 and `abilityBlock` 3→2 while going silent nowhere. **AND THE GUARD'S OWN IDENTITY CHECK WAS THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE FIX**: `verify()` decided *"is this the same fixture?"* from ROUND and the scenario LABELS, so editing a body inside a scenario that keeps its name reported every moved column as *"these features changed MEANING"* — falsely accusing `board.js` of a change it did not make, and collapsing the refit/restamp distinction the whole file turns on. It now carries a BODY digest, backward-compatible with older stamps | closed 2026-08-13 — `docs/MEASURE.md` |
| #266 | **FORTY-ONE ILLEGAL FIXTURE DECLARATIONS ARE BASELINED AND OWED A REPAIR BATCH — OPENED 2026-08-13 BY #265.** They are not MEASURE's files and each repair changes what its scenario measures, so folding forty into the pass that ADDS the check makes both unattributable — the same reasoning already recorded in `data/fixture-learnset-baseline.json`, of which this baseline is the superset. **BY FILE — verdicts touching each, which SUM ABOVE 41 because one sentence can be produced in several files (Snorlax|Swords Dance in eleven places across two): `tests/staged_board.js` 15, `tests/test-protocol-trace.js` 12** (several are mainline recollections the mod does not carry — *"Gengar can't have Levitate"*, Champions gives it Cursed Body; plus the `'dampro'` typo), **`tests/staged_status_counters.js` 7, `tests/test-perish-song.js` 3, `tests/test-volatile-duration.js` 3, `tests/test-game-diff.js` 2, `tests/test-switch-carry.js` 2, `tests/test-click-censoring.js` 2**, and one each in `test-speed-tie.js`, `test-nature-differential.js`, `probe_volatile_leaves.js`. **THE COST OF EACH IS THE SAME SHAPE**: the body was chosen for a typing, a bulk, a speed or a pairing with another test, and a legal replacement moves the board rather than renaming it — `test-perish-song.js` and `test-volatile-duration.js` deliberately share bodies so a duration change cannot pass in one and fail in the other, and `test-speed-tie.js` is pinned to four 100-base-Speed bodies. **NOTHING MAY BE RE-BASELINED INTO SILENCE**: the gate fails on a baselined verdict that stops being produced, so a repair must remove its entry | open — route by file: staged boards and the differential fixtures to `@engine`, the rest to whoever owns the test |
| #267 | **A STATUS IS SEEDED AND THE COUNTER HANGING OFF IT IS NOT, SO SLEEP RESTARTS AND TOXIC RESETS — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE CLOSING #248.** The seed now carries `status` onto every body, active and benched, and carries NOTHING about how far through that status the body is. `buildMon` returns `slp: 0` and the toxic ramp starts at stage 0, so a body two turns into a one-or-two-turn sleep wakes late in every playout, and a `tox` at stage 5 chips at stage 1. **THE ENGINE IS NOT THE PROBLEM AND ALREADY HAS THE HARDER HALF RIGHT**: medicham2's 2026-08-12 note walks the format's own condition table and records that `par/brn/psn/frz/slp` have no switch handler so their counters CARRY OVER, while `tox.onSwitchIn` sets `stage = 0` so the ramp RESTARTS — it models both. **THE BOARD RECORDS ONLY THE STATUS NAME.** Both worlds can supply the counter: the protocol's `|-status|` gives the turn it landed and the store's `x` event does too, so the quantity is `turn - turnItLanded`, derived once on the board rather than a second counter per consumer. Sizing: `x` events appear in the bo3 store at scale and every one of them is a body whose status clock the seed sets to zero | open — DEFECT, board.js + rollout_leaf.js |
| #268 | **THE BOARD GIVES A PERMANENT HAZARD A DURATION AND COUNTS NO LAYERS, SO THE SEED CAN ONLY SEE IT BRIEFLY AND ONLY ONE DEEP — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE CLOSING #249.** Derived from the format, not recalled: Stealth Rock, Spikes, Sticky Web and Toxic Spikes carry NO `condition.duration`, because they last until they are removed. `board.startSide(side, cond, duration)` defaults an absent duration to `turn + 1`, so in the OFFLINE path — the fitter's board — a hazard is up for exactly one turn; the live path passes `fieldDuration`'s default of 5 and is wrong for longer. Measured on a fixture: laid `true`, one `endTurn()` later `false`, while the real rocks are still there. **SO `deadSide` GOES BACK TO ZERO AND THE MODEL RE-LAYS STEALTH ROCK**, which is the same symptom #254 measured for the write-only variant and the reason that row insists the write and the reads move together. Layers are the second half: `sideConditions` is a Map of expiry, not a count, so a seeded Spikes or Toxic Spikes is always ONE layer and the difference between one and two Toxic Spikes is poisoned against badly poisoned. **THIS IS NOT SEARCH'S**: fixing it moves `deadSide` and `setupTurns`, which are fitted FEATURES, so it invalidates `data/policy-weights.json` and belongs with the refit. `tests/test-rollout-seed.js` §6 prints the fixture's before/after on every run so the day it closes is visible | open — DEFECT, board.js (fit-invalidating) |
| #269 | **EVERY DURABLE VOLATILE IS STILL UNSEEDED, AND THE LIVE BOARD ALREADY HAS SOME OF THEM — SPLIT OUT OF #250 BY SEARCH 2026-08-13.** The seed carries HP, status, stages, item, ability, moves, PP, the Protect streak (mine), the move-action count and the entry snapshot. It carries NONE of: choice lock (`_lock`/`_lockT`), Encore (`_encoreMove`), Disable, Taunt, a multi-turn lock (`_mtLock`), a charge turn (`_charging`), Substitute (`_sub`), Leech Seed (`_seededBy`), a partial trap (`_trap`), Recharge, the perish count, or Gigaton Hammer's lockout. **`magnemite.js` ALREADY WRITES `board.startVolatile`/`endVolatile` FROM `|-start|`/`|-end|` WITH A DURATION** (Taunt 3, Encore 3, Disable 5, Throat Chop 2, Yawn 2, Heal Block 5, Perish Song), so the live board holds a real answer that the seed throws away. **THE FIRST TASK IS A VOCABULARY CHECK, NOT A PATCH:** medicham2's `_vol` is a plain map on the body and IS writable from `rollout_leaf` with no engine change, but its keys come from a TAG PARAM while the board's come from `move.volatileStatus`, and a key that does not match seeds a volatile nothing ever reads — silently, which is precisely the terrain-dialect defect MILTANK.md §3.7 records. **AND THE FOE'S PROTECT STREAK IS IN THE WRONG PLACE ENTIRELY**: `buildSide(board, foe, dex, stats)` takes no `protectTurns`, and `mag_bot.js`'s tracker is gated `if (mine)` so the opponent's consecutive Protects are never counted at all. It should not be caller state: `board.noteMove` already reads `move.stallingMove`, so a consecutive counter there serves both sides, both worlds, and removes a second source for one fact | open — DEFECT, rollout_leaf.js + board.js + mag_bot.js |
| #270 | **THE SEEDED FIELD HAS NO CLOCK, SO A WEATHER WITH ONE TURN LEFT LASTS THE WHOLE PLAYOUT — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE SWEEPING THE SEED.** `applyField` sets `S.field.weather` and `S.field.terrain` and never `weatherT`/`terrainT`, and the engine's tick is `if (field.weatherT > 0 && --field.weatherT <= 0)` — so **ZERO MEANS NEVER EXPIRES**. A sun the real board has two turns of runs for sixty in every rollout, mispricing every Fire move, every Solar Beam, every weather-scaled ability and every Aurora Veil for the whole playout. **THE SAME FILE ALREADY DOES IT CORRECTLY ONE FUNCTION AWAY**: `applyMegaWeather` sets `weatherT` from `MEDI.weatherTurns(w, item, TAGS)`, which reads Heat/Damp/Icy/Smooth Rock off the body — so the seed's two weather paths disagree with each other, which is the shape that produced the mega-weather defect in the first place. **THE TWO HALVES ARE NOT EQUALLY BLOCKED:** the terrain clock IS on the board today (`pseudoWeather` holds an expiry, and `board.fieldLeft` reads it back), so that half is three lines; the WEATHER clock is not (`setWeather` takes no duration and records none), so the board must first learn it — from `|-weather|` plus the dex duration plus the holder's rock, which is exactly what `weatherTurns` already computes. **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED IN THE #247–#250 BATCH**: it is one fact, half of it needs board work, and landing half of it inside a four-row batch is what makes the batch's own result unattributable | open — DEFECT, rollout_leaf.js + board.js |
| #271 | **A KNOCKED-OFF ITEM IS STILL ON THE BOARD'S BODY, SO EVERY DAMAGE FEATURE AND EVERY ROLLOUT KEEPS APPLYING AN ITEM THAT IS GONE — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13, AND IT IS THE CLAUDE.md LESSON BROKEN IN THE PLACE THAT LESSON WAS WRITTEN ABOUT.** `board.switchIn` copies the item off the SHEET onto the slot object; `board.noteItem` — the one thing `|-item|`/`|-enditem|` reaches — writes only `itemNow`, and `sheetItem()` is the only reader of that. **`dmgMon` reads `mon.item`.** Measured, not argued: a Garchomp declared with a Life Orb, then `noteItem('p1','garchomp','')`, gives `sheetItem -> ''` and `slot.item -> lifeorb` and `dmgMon(...).item -> lifeorb`. So a Life Orb, a Choice Scarf, an eaten Sitrus Berry and a spent Focus Sash all keep applying — in the damage numbers MAG scores with AND in every seeded playout. CLAUDE.md states the mechanism exactly: *"the damage and speed calculations keep applying a … Choice Scarf or Life Orb that is gone — and those apply at ANY hp"*. **SIZED FROM THE STORE (a FLOOR, because the store records no consumption at all): 2,375 uses of an item-removing move across 14,102 open-sheet games, 10.62% of games contain one, and 5.95% of decision points come after one has been clicked.** The live path sees far more, because `|-enditem|` also fires for every eaten berry and every spent Sash. **NOT SEARCH'S TO FIX**: `switchIn` and `dmgMon` are what the weights were fitted through, so correcting the item changes fitted feature values and belongs with the refit. The right shape is that the slot's item is READ through `sheetItem()` rather than copied at switch-in — one fact, one function | open — DEFECT, board.js (fit-invalidating) |

The recurring failure of this project, kept together so the pattern stays visible.

| model | state |
|---|---|
| **DODUO** | fitted, refitted, +2.1 top-1 over independent choice, **never read by the search** (#53) |

| **the Nash solver** | verified on RPS and a 2×2, in Python, unreachable from the bot (#41) |

Now derived from the condition itself (duration, the `random(5,7)` range, the Grip Claw branch, `onStart`'s `boundDivisor`) and **failing closed**. Red shown on the frozen pre-fix release. Whole-game differential unmoved at 65/107. **Remaining clusters BY CORPUS WEIGHT, which is not the order they look biggest in — `encore` alone is 6,102 uses, 55% of what is left, while the whole lock-in family is 101:** 13 `move/plain-attack` (of which outrage/petaldance/ragingfury/thrash/uproar are one lock-in family), 4 remaining `move/volatile`, 9 `move/generic-status`, 6 `move/boosts-target`, 3 `move/multihit` (Triple Axel, Scale Shot, Dragon Darts — three DIFFERENT mechanics), 3 `move/fixed-damage` | open — `docs/ENGINE.md` |
| #70 | **TWO USAGE COUNTS EXIST AND NEITHER KNOWS ABOUT THE OTHER — but the engine queue is NOT where it bites.** `data/meta-usage.json` (what CHOMP reads) filters hard: collected 39,792 → **after_bot_filter 13,263** → behavioural bots 10,480 → forfeits → min turns → full bring → **7,123 clean**. `data/tags.json` (which every "N uses" in this project is quoted from) reads `loadCorpus({scope:'all'})` across `games.bo3` + `games.ots` + `games.ladder` — 11,429 games, 137,148 sheet entries, **no bot filter and no quality filter**. **CORRECTED 2026-08-10 BY WILL, and the correction is the important half:** *"it doesnt really matter because its not like bots can break the rules, the moves, mons, and items they use we still need to have in medicham right"*. Exactly right. A bot cannot click an illegal move, so every entity it uses is one MEDICHAM must handle anyway — and the MEDICHAM gate demands **zero** DIFFER and **zero** DID-NOT-FIRE, so the count changes the ORDER of work and never the destination. The unfiltered count is arguably the CORRECT one for an engine queue: it measures exposure. **Where it does still bite is the OPPONENT MODEL** — what a human will bring and click — which is what the bot filter exists for and what CHOMP consumes. So this is a MEASURE item about `meta-usage.json`, not an ENGINE item about the queue. *(An earlier draft of this row claimed three sources disagreeing 13x, citing a scan of `games.ladder.jsonl` alone that found 15 Iron Ball rows against tags.json's 139. That scan was WRONG — ladder alone carries sheets on 1.7% of sides because the sheets live in the bo3 and ots files. The 139 is sound. Retracted.)* | open — MEASURE, not ENGINE |

Four false findings across 2,049 uses, every one of them reported as an engine bug.
